</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 24pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Stock-based compensation</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company accounts for its stock-based compensation issued to
directors, officers, and employees in accordance with the
provisions of Accounting Principles Board (&#147;APB&#148;)
Opinion No.&nbsp;25, <I>Accounting for Stock Issued to
Employees</I>, and related interpretations. Under APB
No.&nbsp;25, compensation expense is recognized if an
option&#146;s exercise price on the measurement date is below
the fair market value of the Company&#146;s common stock.
Pursuant to FASB Interpretation No.&nbsp;28, Accounting for
Stock Appreciation Rights and Other Variable Stock Option or
Award Plans, the Company amortizes the compensation expense over
the vesting period on an accelerated basis. During the third
quarter of 2005, the Company recorded a stock-based compensation
charge of $0.8&nbsp;million from the acceleration of vesting
terms of certain options. These modifications were unrelated to
the restructuring charge as noted in Note&nbsp;12.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;123, <I>Accounting for Stock-Based
Compensation</I>, requires pro forma information regarding net
income (loss) as if the Company had accounted for its stock
options and restricted stock granted under the fair value method
prescribed by SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;123. The following is the pro
forma disclosure and the related impact on net income and net
income per common share for the years ended December&nbsp;31,
2005, 2004, and 2003 if the Company had applied the fair value
recognition provisions of SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;123 to stock-based
employee compensation (in thousands, except per share amounts):
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
On December&nbsp;1, 2003, the Company acquired Wise Solutions,
Inc. (&#147;Wise Solutions&#148;)(&#147;the Wise
Acquisition&#148;). Accordingly, the operations of Wise
Solutions have been included in the accompanying consolidated
statements of operations for the Company since the Wise
Acquisition was closed. At the effective time of the Wise
Acquisition (the &#147;Effective Time&#148;), (i)&nbsp;Wise
Solutions became a wholly owned subsidiary of the Company;
(ii)&nbsp;the former shareholders of Wise Solutions received, in
exchange for their shares of common stock of Wise Solutions in
the aggregate approximately (a)&nbsp;$25.2&nbsp;million in cash
and (b)&nbsp;348,794&nbsp;shares of the Company&#146;s common
stock; and (iii)&nbsp;all unexpired and unexercised options to
purchase shares of Wise Solutions common stock granted under the
stock option plan and agreements of Wise Solutions outstanding
immediately prior to the Effective Time were, in connection with
the Wise Acquisition, accelerated, fully vested and terminated
in exchange for an aggregate payment of $6.3&nbsp;million. The
348,794&nbsp;shares of the Company&#146;s common stock issued in
connection with the Wise Acquisition were valued at
approximately $11.9&nbsp;million based upon the closing price on
December&nbsp;1, 2003, which was the date the acquisition was
closed. Additionally, direct acquisition costs incurred as of
December&nbsp;31, 2003, totaled $339,000.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
During the first quarter of 2004, the Company paid additional
legal fees directly associated with the acquisition of Wise
Solutions in the amount of $84,000, which resulted in additional
goodwill. In addition, during the second quarter of 2004, the
Company booked additional goodwill related to the acquisition of
Wise Solutions of $1.1&nbsp;million. Of this increase, $664,000
was related to the voluntary termination of five employees of
Wise Solutions, which was accounted for in accordance with
EITF&nbsp;95-3: <I>Recognition of Liabilities in Connection with
a Purchase Business Combination</I>, and $430,000 was the result
of a pre-acquisition contingency related to InstallShield&#146;s
suit against Wise Solutions for which the Company obtained
information during the discovery process that enabled the
Company to estimate the fair value of this pre-acquisition
contingency.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Total consideration for the Wise Acquisition was
$44.9&nbsp;million. The purchase price was allocated among the
tangible and identifiable intangible assets and liabilities
assumed based upon their estimated fair
</DIV>

</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Notes to Consolidated Financial
Statements&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
options for $6.3&nbsp;million and incurred acquisition costs of
$3.1&nbsp;million for a total purchase price of
$77.1&nbsp;million. A portion of the consideration has been
placed in escrow to satisfy certain indemnification obligations
of the former shareholders of Pedestal. The purchase price
includes purchased goodwill of $52.1&nbsp;million. The Company
will test the goodwill for impairment at least annually.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Upon consummation of the Pedestal acquisition, the Company
immediately charged to expense $1.6&nbsp;million representing
acquired in-process research and development that had not yet
reached technological feasibility and had no alternative future
use. The amount of purchase price allocated to acquired
in-process research and development was determined through
established valuation techniques. The value was determined by
estimating viable products, estimating the resulting net cash
flows from such products, and discounting the net cash flows
back to their present value. The discount rate included a factor
that took into account the uncertainty surrounding the
successful development of the acquired in-process research and
development.
</DIV>

The Company assumed all unvested Pedestal options as of the date
of the acquisition, which were converted into options to
purchase approximately 257,172 of the Company&#146;s common
stock. The fair value of the options assumed was determined
using the Black-Scholes option pricing model with the following
inputs: risk-free interest rate of 4.17%, an expected life of
4&nbsp;years, volatility of 131%, zero dividend rate, and the
fair value of the Company&#146;s common stock as of
March&nbsp;29, 2005. The intrinsic value of these options on the
date of the Pedestal acquisition totaled $3.2&nbsp;million and
will be recognized as compensation expense in future periods.
The Company recognized $2.4&nbsp;million of compensation expense
related to these options in the year ended December&nbsp;31,
2005.

been prepared for comparative purposes only and do not purport
to be indicative of what would have occurred had the acquisition

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>BridgeWater Technologies</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
On August&nbsp;31, 2004, the Company purchased certain assets
and assumed certain liabilities from BridgeWater Technologies,
Inc. (&#147;BridgeWater&#148;), (the &#147;BridgeWater
Acquisition&#148;). The 4,154&nbsp;shares of the Company&#146;s
common stock issued in connection with the BridgeWater
Acquisition were valued at approximately $0.1&nbsp;million based
upon the closing price on August&nbsp;31, 2004, the date the
Company signed the asset purchase agreement with BridgeWater. In
addition, 12,463&nbsp;shares of the Company&#146;s common stock
were issued as prepaid compensation expense and valued at
approximately $0.3&nbsp;million were amortized over the
12&nbsp;month service period. BridgeWater was a producer of
network device discovery, provisioning and configuration
technology.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Operating leases</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company is committed under non-cancelable operating leases
involving office facilities and office and computer equipment.
Rent expense for non-cancelable operating leases was
$4.1&nbsp;million, $3.4&nbsp;million and $2.4&nbsp;million for
the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, 2004 and 2003,
respectively. As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, aggregate future
lease commitments are $4&nbsp;million, $1&nbsp;million,
$364,000, $35,000, and $0 for the years ending December&nbsp;31,
2006, 2007, 2008, 2009 and 2010, respectively.
</DIV>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>(5)</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Equity transactions</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Convertible Preferred Stock</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
In May 2000, the Company amended its articles of incorporation
to authorize 4,000,000&nbsp;shares of preferred stock, of which
2,000,000&nbsp;shares were designated Series&nbsp;A Preferred
and 2,000,000&nbsp;shares were designated Series&nbsp;B
Preferred. The Series&nbsp;A Preferred and Series&nbsp;B
Preferred had priority over common stock with respect to
dividend rights and liquidation rights. The holders of
Series&nbsp;A Preferred and Series&nbsp;B Preferred were
entitled to receive dividends at the rate of $0.45&nbsp;per
share per annum on Series&nbsp;A Preferred and at the rate of
$0.70&nbsp;per share on Series&nbsp;B Preferred when and if
declared by the board of directors. The preferred stockholders
had the right to one vote for each share of common stock into
which such preferred stock would be converted with full voting
rights and powers of the common stockholders.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
In the event of any voluntary or involuntary liquidation,
dissolution or winding up of the Company, each holder of
preferred stock was entitled to receive an amount equal to the
original purchase price plus declared but unpaid dividends. Upon
completion of the preferred distribution, the remaining assets
of the Company would be distributed among the holders of
preferred and common stock pro rata based on the number of
shares of common stock held by each, assuming conversion of all
preferred stock. A change in control, as defined, as a result of
any reorganization, merger or consolidation, or a sale of
substantially all of the Company&#146;s assets was deemed to be
a liquidation. The holders of preferred stock could convert
their shares of preferred stock into common stock on a one for
one basis, subject to certain anti-dilution provisions.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Effective as of the completion of the Company&#146;s initial
public offering of its common stock (the &#147;IPO&#148;), each
outstanding share of Series&nbsp;A Preferred and the New
Series&nbsp;B Preferred converted into one share of common
stock, and each outstanding share of Series&nbsp;C Preferred
converted into one share of Class&nbsp;B Stock. Following such
conversions, the Company&#146;s Certificate of Incorporation was
amended and restated to delete all references to the
Series&nbsp;A Preferred, the New Series&nbsp;B Preferred and the
Series&nbsp;C Preferred, and 5,000,000&nbsp;shares of
undesignated preferred stock of the Company was authorized. The
Company&#146;s board of directors has the authority, without any
further vote or action by the Company&#146;s stockholders, to
issue from time to time preferred stock in one or more series
and to fix the price, rights, preferences, privileges and
restrictions thereof. The authorized shares of common stock of
the Company were also increased to 100,000,000&nbsp;shares. All
outstanding shares of Class&nbsp;B Stock automatically converted
into common stock in May 2003.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Common stock</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
In May 2002, the Company completed the sale and issuance of
5,000,000&nbsp;shares of common stock in its IPO at a price of
$10.00&nbsp;per share. Net proceeds to the Company after
underwriting discounts and commissions and direct offering costs
approximated $43.8&nbsp;million. As noted above, with the
completion of the IPO, the Series&nbsp;A preferred and New
Series&nbsp;B Preferred were converted into 2,111,112 and
2,933,333&nbsp;shares of common stock, respectively, and the
Series&nbsp;C preferred was converted into 258,064&nbsp;shares
of Class&nbsp;B Stock.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
In August 2003, the Company completed a follow-on public
offering of 3,750,000&nbsp;shares of common stock at a price of
$18.75&nbsp;per share.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company granted Canopy, a related party at the time, a
five-year warrant to purchase&nbsp;272,728&nbsp;shares of common
stock at $5.50&nbsp;per share. In February 2002, Canopy
exercised the warrant and the Company received proceeds of
$1,500,000.
</DIV>

    <TD><B>(6)</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Stock Option Plan</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>1998 Stock Plan</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company has adopted the 1998 Stock Option Plan (the
&#147;1998 Plan&#148;). The 1998 Plan provides for the granting
of non-qualified stock options to purchase shares of the
Company&#146;s common stock. The 1998 Plan is administered by
the board of directors. Under the 1998 Plan, the board of
directors could grant up to 4,325,000 options to employees,
directors and consultants. Options granted under the 1998 Plan
are subject to expiration and vesting terms as determined by the
board of directors. In February 2002, the board of directors
determined to discontinue granting stock options under the 1998
Plan and to retire any shares of common stock reserved for
issuance under such plan and not subject to outstanding stock
options. At that time, 4,197,058 options had been granted under
the 1998 Plan.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Under the terms of the 1998 Plan, the options generally expire
10&nbsp;years after the date of grant or within three months of
termination and generally vest as to 25&nbsp;percent of the
shares underlying the options at the end of each one year period
over four years and are exercisable as they vest. The 1998 Plan
contains certain restrictions and limitations, including the
Company&#146;s right of first refusal on the transfer or sale of
shares issued upon exercise of vested options.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>2002 Stock Plan</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company&#146;s board of directors adopted and the
Company&#146;s stockholders approved the 2002 Stock Plan (the
&#147;2002 Plan&#148;) in January 2002. The 2002 Plan provides
for the granting of incentive stock options to the
Company&#146;s employees, and for the grant of nonstatutory
stock options and stock purchase rights to the Company&#146;s
employees, directors and consultants. A total of
1,180,762&nbsp;shares of common stock were initially reserved
for issuance pursuant to the 2002 Plan, and the 2002 Plan
provides for annual increases in the number of shares available
on the first day of each year, beginning in 2003, equal to the
lesser of 3&nbsp;percent of the outstanding shares of common
stock on the first day of the applicable year,
1,000,000&nbsp;shares, or another amount as the Company&#146;s
board of directors may determine. The 2002 Plan is administered
by the board of directors or, by committees appointed by the
board of directors (&#147;Administrators&#148;). The
Administrator has the power to determine the terms of the
options and stock purchase rights granted, including the
exercise price, the number of shares subject to each option or
stock purchase right, the exercisability of the options and
stock purchase rights and the form of consideration payable upon
exercise.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>2005 Stock Plan</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company assumed the 2005 Stock Plan (the &#147;2005
Plan,&#148; formerly known as the Pedestal Software, Inc. 2002
Stock Option and Incentive Plan) during the Pedestal
acquisition. A total of 503,672&nbsp;shares of common stock were
reserved for issuance pursuant to the 2005 Plan at the time it
was assumed by the Company. The Company also assumed all of the
then unvested options granted pursuant to the 2005 Plan as of
the date of the acquisition, which were converted into options
to purchase approximately 257,172 of the Company&#146;s common
stock. The 2005 Plan provides for the granting of incentive
stock options to the Company&#146;s employees, other than those
employees who were employed by the Company or one of the
Company&#146;s subsidiaries (other than Pedestal) at the time of
the Pedestal acquisition and for the grant of nonstatutory stock
options, restricted stock, stock purchase rights, phantom stock
and other awards based on the Company&#146;s common stock to the
Company&#146;s employees, other than those employees who were
employed by the Company or one of the Company&#146;s
subsidiaries (other than Pedestal) at the time of the Pedestal
acquisition, directors and consultants. The 2005 Plan is
administered by the board of directors or by committees
appointed by the board of directors
(&#147;Administrators&#148;). The Administrators have the power
to determine the terms of the awards granted, including the
exercise price, the number of shares subject to each award, the
exercisability of the awards and the form of consideration.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Stock-based compensation</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Prior to the Company&#146;s IPO, certain options with exercise
prices below the estimated fair market value on the grant date
resulting in deferred compensation. The deferred compensation
has been recorded as a component of stockholders&#146; equity
and is being recognized over the vesting period of the
underlying stock options, using the accelerated method under
FIN&nbsp;28.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>2002 Employee Stock Purchase Plan</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
In February 2002, the Company&#146;s board of directors adopted
the 2002 Employee Stock Purchase Plan (the &#147;ESPP&#148;),
which became effective upon the completion of the Company&#146;s
initial public offering. A total of 500,000&nbsp;shares of
common stock were initially reserved for issuance under the
ESPP, and the ESPP provides for annual increases in the number
of shares available for issuance on the first day of each year,
beginning with 2003, equal to the lesser of 2&nbsp;percent of
the outstanding shares of the Company&#146;s common stock on the
first day of the applicable year, 750,000&nbsp;shares, or
another amount as the Company&#146;s board of directors may
determine. The Company&#146;s board of directors or a committee
established by the board of
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">F-23
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">
<B>ALTIRIS, INC. AND SUBSIDIARIES</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Notes to Consolidated Financial
Statements&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
directors will administer the ESPP and will have authority to
interpret the terms of the ESPP and determine eligibility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The ESPP is intended to qualify under Section&nbsp;423 of the
Internal Revenue Code and contains consecutive,
<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">6-month</FONT> offering
periods. The offering periods generally start on the first
trading day on or after February 1 and August&nbsp;1 of each
year, except for the first such offering period which commenced
on the first trading day on or after the effective date of the
IPO and ended on February&nbsp;1, 2003. All eligible employees
were automatically enrolled in the first offering period.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Participants can purchase common stock through payroll
deductions of up to 10&nbsp;percent of their eligible
compensation which includes a participant&#146;s base salary,
overtime and shift premiums and commissions, but excludes all
other compensation. A participant could purchase a maximum of
1,125&nbsp;shares during the first offering period under the
ESPP and may purchase a maximum of 750&nbsp;shares during each
subsequent <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">six-month</FONT>
offering period. Amounts deducted and accumulated for the
participant are used to purchase shares of common stock at the
end of each six-month offering period. The price is
85&nbsp;percent of the lower of the fair market value of the
common stock at the beginning of an offering period or at the
end of an offering period. Participants may end their
participation at any time during an offering period, and will be
paid their payroll deductions to date. Participation ends three
months following termination of employment. A participant may
not transfer rights granted under the ESPP other than by will,
the laws of descent and distribution or as otherwise provided
under the ESPP.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; color: #000000; background: #ffffff;"></DIV>

    <TD width="95%"></TD>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>(7)</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Altiris 401(k) Plan</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company adopted the Altiris, Inc. 401(k) Plan (the
&#147;401(k) Plan&#148;) in February 2002. The 401(k) Plan
covers all Altiris U.S.&nbsp;employees who meet certain
requirements. The Plan allows the Company to contribute an
amount equal to 50% of the first 6% of eligible compensation
that the employee contributes.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company&#146;s matching contributions to the 401(k) Plan was
$1.1&nbsp;million, $0.8&nbsp;million, and $0.6&nbsp;million for
the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, 2004, and 2003,
respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company has not provided for U.S.&nbsp;deferred income taxes
or foreign withholding taxes on the undistributed earnings of
its foreign subsidiaries since these earnings are intended to be
reinvested indefinitely. It is not practical to estimate the
amount of additional taxes that might be payable on such
undistributed earnings.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
From inception through December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company has
generated net operating losses (&#147;NOL&#148;) for federal
income tax reporting purposes of $7.0&nbsp;million, which will
begin to expire in 2021. This consists of $1.1&nbsp;million and
$5.9&nbsp;million from the acquisitions of Tonic and Pedestal.
The Company has various state net operating loss carryovers
which expire depending on the rules of the various states to
which the loss is allocated. The Company recorded
$2.1&nbsp;million of benefit as an increase to additional
paid-in capital
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">F-25

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">
<B>ALTIRIS, INC. AND SUBSIDIARIES</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Notes to Consolidated Financial
Statements&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
in the current year resulting from exercise of stock options of
$5.4&nbsp;million and the reversal of valuation allowance from
NOLs generated from stock option deduction of $2.4&nbsp;million.
In the year ending December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company generated
$4.4&nbsp;million of net operating losses in certain foreign
jurisdictions. Other than $108,000 of benefit allowed for
carryback in France, the Company has recorded a valuation
allowance against these foreign losses due to the uncertainty of
future taxable income in such foreign jurisdictions.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The valuation allowance of $1.2&nbsp;million relates to a
portion of the Tonic NOL carryforwards. Should the Company
ultimately realize a benefit from these NOLs, the reversal of
the valuation allowance would result in a provision benefit for
the Company.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Internal Revenue Code contains provisions that reduce or
limit the availability and utilization of NOL carryforwards if
certain changes in ownership have taken place or will take
place. As a result of an ownership change associated with the
acquisition of Tonic, utilization of Tonic NOL carryforwards,
arising prior to the ownership change date, will be limited to
an annual amount not to exceed the value of the Company on the
ownership change date multiplied by the Federal long-term
tax-exempt rate. If the annual limitation of approximately
$230,000 is not utilized in any particular year, it will remain
available on a cumulative basis through the expiration date of
the applicable NOL carryforwards.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Deferred income taxes are determined based on the differences
between the financial reporting and income tax bases of assets
and liabilities using enacted income tax rates which will apply
when the differences are expected to be settled or realized. The
significant components of the Company&#146;s deferred tax assets
(liabilities)&nbsp;as of December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004 are as
follows (in thousands):
</DIV>

<CENTER>
<TABLE width="100%" align="center" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="0" border="0" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt; ">

<TR style="font-size: 1pt;">
    <TD width="77%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="4%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="4%">&nbsp;</TD>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">F-26
<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">
<B>ALTIRIS, INC. AND SUBSIDIARIES</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Notes to Consolidated Financial
Statements&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 18pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;109, <I>Accounting for Income Taxes
</I>requires that a valuation allowance be established when it
is more likely than not that all or a portion of a deferred tax
asset will not be realized. The Company previously recorded a
full valuation allowance for all of its U.S.&nbsp;net deferred
income tax assets due to the uncertainty of realization of the
assets based upon a number of factors including the limited
operating history of the Company, the high volatility and
uncertainty of the industry in which the Company operates, and a
significant number of outstanding stock options which were
expected to result in a significant reduction of future taxable
income when the employees exercise such options. At
December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company and the Company&#146;s
results of operations have mitigated this uncertainty. Thus, the
remaining valuation allowance is due solely to the NOL
carryforwards of Tonic of $1.2&nbsp;million and to foreign
losses of $1.3&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; color: #000000; background: #ffffff;"></DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">

<TR>
    <TD width="5%"></TD>
    <TD width="95%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>(9)</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Commitments and contingencies</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Indemnifications</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Certain of the Company&#146;s negotiated license agreements
include indemnification provisions for claims from third-parties
relating to the Company&#146;s intellectual property. Such
indemnification provisions are accounted for in accordance with
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;5, <I>Accounting for Contingencies.</I> At
December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company is not aware of any material
liabilities arising from these indemnifications.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Legal matters</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
In December 1999, the Company commenced a patent infringement
suit against Symantec Corporation, or Symantec, in the United
States District Court for the District of Utah (the &#147;Utah
Action&#148;). In April 2004, Symantec and its wholly owned
subsidiary, PowerQuest Corporation, or PowerQuest, jointly
commenced a patent infringement suit against the Company in the
United States District Court for the Eastern District of Texas
(the &#147;Texas Action&#148;). In May 2005, the Company,
Symantec, and PowerQuest entered into a settlement agreement
that resolved all claims brought by the parties in the Utah
Action and the Texas Action, respectively, and both cases have
been dismissed with prejudice.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company is involved in claims that arise in the ordinary
course of business. In accordance with the Statement of
Financial Accounting Standards No.&nbsp;5, &#147;Accounting
Contingencies,&#148; the Company makes a provision for a
liability when it is both probable that the liability has been
incurred and the amount of the loss can be reasonably estimated.
The Company believes it has adequate provisions for any such
matters. The Company reviews these provisions at least quarterly
and adjusts these provisions to reflect the impacts of
negotiations, settlements, rulings, advice of legal counsel, and
other information and events pertaining to a particular case.
Litigation is inherently unpredictable. However, in the
Company&#146;s opinion, the ultimate disposition of these
matters will not have a material adverse effect on the
Company&#146;s results of operations or financial position.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B><I>Concentration of credit risk and significant
customers</I></B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company offers credit terms on the sale of its software
products to certain customers. The Company generally performs
ongoing credit evaluations of its customers&#146; financial
condition and requires no collateral from its customers. The
Company maintains an allowance for doubtful accounts receivable
based upon the expected collectibility of all accounts
receivable. For the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, 2004 and
2003,
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">F-27

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company leases from the Canopy Group, Inc.
(&#147;Canopy&#148;), a prior shareholder, office space and
related furniture and office equipment. As of December&nbsp;31,
2005, the lease for the Company&#146;s current corporate
headquarters provides for monthly rent of $125,000 and expires
in December 2006. During the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2003,
the Company incurred rent expense to Canopy of
$1.3&nbsp;million. As of December&nbsp;31, 2004, Canopy is no
longer considered a related party.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
CEW, an entity owned by an executive officer of the Company,
purchased approximately $447,000 of the Company&#146;s product
during the year ended 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The Company utilizes the services of an outsourced vending
company for internal use. An executive officer of the Company is
related to the individual specifically accountable for the
Company&#146;s account. For the year ended 2005, the Company
paid costs of $78,000 for these vending services.
</DIV>

    <TD>
    <B>Segment, geographic and customer information</B></TD>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;131, <I>Disclosures about Segments of an
Enterprise and Related Information.</I> SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;131
establishes standards for public enterprises to report
information about operating segments in annual financial
statements and requires that those enterprises report selected
information about operating segments in interim reports.
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;131 also establishes standards for related
disclosures about products and services, geographic areas and
major customers. The Company operates as one segment, the
development and marketing of IT service-oriented management
software products and services.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Revenue from customers located outside the United States
accounted for 37&nbsp;percent, 36&nbsp;percent and
33&nbsp;percent of total revenue during the years ended
December&nbsp;31, 2005, 2004 and 2003, respectively. The
majority of international sales have been made in Europe and
Canada. There were no significant long-lived assets held outside
the United States.
</DIV>

    <TD><B>(12)</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Restructuring charge</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
During 2005, the Company recorded a restructuring charge and
accrual of $2.1&nbsp;million affecting continuing operations and
related to involuntary employee termination benefits for
individuals throughout the Company&#146;s workforce, including
$0.2&nbsp;million of stock-based compensation related to the
accelerated vesting of certain stock options.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
Pursuant to the restructuring plan, the Company notified 86
employees that their employment would be terminated. As of
December&nbsp;31, 2005, 84 of these employees had left the
Company&#146;s employment. As of December&nbsp;31, 2005,
$2.1&nbsp;million had been paid as termination benefits and
related costs pursuant to the restructuring.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
As part of the restructuring plan, the Company determined that
office space in two locations in Landau Germany were no longer
required. This office space, as of December&nbsp;31, 2005, was
no longer in use and, therefore, $71,000 of costs were accrued.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
The following summarizes the restructuring plan activity as it
relates to the employee severance and other related benefits as
of December&nbsp;31, 2005 (in thousands):
</DIV>
